<p class="sx-lead">Colour scales for charts and anything else - perceptual, colour-blind safe, sequential, diverging and cyclic.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="amber">
  <h2><a href="?palette/amber">Amber</a></h2>
  <p>Warm scale from cream to dark amber. The relative luminance strictly decreases, including sRGB interpolated samples. PAD original, GPL-3.0; no claim of perceptual uniformity.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="batlow">
  <h2><a href="?palette/batlow">batlow</a></h2>
  <p>Fabio Crameri&#039;s sequential scale batlow, from a dark navy through teal, a moss green, olive and ochre to salmon and a pale pink: perceptually uniform, its lightness rising all the way, so it prints as a ramp in grey, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are. The 256 colours of batlow.txt in his Scientific colour maps 8.0.1 (Zenodo, doi:10.5281/zenodo.8409685), each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded. Copyright (c) 2020 Fabio Crameri, MIT licence.</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">

<section class="sx-kind" id="blue-orange">
  <h2><a href="?palette/blue-orange">Blue-Orange</a></h2>
  <p>Deviations around a neutral #f2f2f2 midpoint. Blue negative and orange positive arms have closely matched relative luminance at corresponding anchors (difference below 0.025). PAD original, GPL-3.0. No claim of universal color-vision accessibility.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="blues">
  <h2><a href="?palette/blues">Blues</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s single-hue sequential scale of nine blues, from nearly white to navy (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</p>

  <h2><a href="?palette/brbg">BrBG</a></h2>

<section class="sx-kind" id="cividis">
  <h2><a href="?palette/cividis">Cividis</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from navy to yellow made to look alike to people with and without a red-green colour vision deficiency (Nu&ntilde;ez, Anderton and Renslow, 2018). From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="dark2">
  <h2><a href="?palette/dark2">Dark2</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s qualitative Dark2, eight deep colours to tell categories apart - teal green, orange, slate violet, magenta, green, mustard, brown and grey (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org): darker and more saturated than its pastels, so thin lines and small marks stay visible on a light surface; ColorBrewer marks its first three safe for colour-blind eyes, four to six possibly, more not. From colorbrewer_schemes.js of ColorBrewer 2.0 (axismaps/colorbrewer), checked against d3-scale-chromatic. Apache-2.0 licence: this product includes color specifications and designs developed by Cynthia Brewer (http://colorbrewer.org/).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="earth">
  <h2><a href="?palette/earth">Earth</a></h2>
  <p>Eight tones of soil, clay, moss and sand, PAD&#039;s own qualitative palette: umber, olive, ochre, slate, sienna, moss, sand and a dark brown - quiet colours for a page in warm tones.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="glasbey">
  <h2><a href="?palette/glasbey">Glasbey</a></h2>
  <p>Thirty-two colours to tell many categories apart, each chosen as far as it can be from all before it, by the method of Chris Glasbey, Gerie van der Heijden, Vivian Toh and others (&quot;Colour displays for categorical images&quot;, Color Research and Application 32.4, 2007): started from white and black, so none is near either, and no grey among them, every colour of a chroma of 20 or more. Past eight the colours are for a map or a legend more than a chart, which has eight slots, and colour-blind eyes will confuse some of them - for them okabe-ito or a Tol palette. The first 32 of the 256 colours of colorcet&#039;s glasbey (glasbey_bw_minc_20, colorcet/__init__.py, HoloViz), each channel k/255, unchanged but for the cut; colorcet, CC BY 4.0 licence.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="greys">
  <h2><a href="?palette/greys">Greys</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s sequential scale of nine greys, from white to black (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="inferno">
  <h2><a href="?palette/inferno">Inferno</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from black through purple, red and orange to a pale yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="magma">
  <h2><a href="?palette/magma">Magma</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from black through purple and red to a pale yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="okabe-ito">
  <h2><a href="?palette/okabe-ito">Okabe-Ito</a></h2>
  <p>The eight colours Masataka Okabe and Kei Ito chose to stay apart for every kind of colour vision (Color Universal Design, 2008): orange, sky blue, bluish green, yellow, blue, vermillion, reddish purple and black - black put last here, where the original lists it first, as it vanishes on a dark surface.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="paired">
  <h2><a href="?palette/paired">Paired</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s qualitative Paired, twelve colours in six pairs, a light and a dark of one hue each - blue, green, red, orange, purple, and a pale yellow with a brown (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org): for categories that come in pairs - last year and this, planned and done - the pair&#039;s hue telling the group and its lightness the member; ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes up to four colours, possibly to eight, more not. From colorbrewer_schemes.js of ColorBrewer 2.0 (axismaps/colorbrewer), checked against d3-scale-chromatic. Apache-2.0 licence: this product includes color specifications and designs developed by Cynthia Brewer (http://colorbrewer.org/).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="phase">
  <h2><a href="?palette/phase">Phase</a></h2>
  <p>Cyclic angle scale; samples stop before the repeated first color. PAD original, GPL-3.0. Interpolation joins the final rose-violet anchor back to the first; no discontinuity at integer turns. No claim of perceptual uniformity.</p>

  <h2><a href="?palette/piyg">PiYG</a></h2>

<section class="sx-kind" id="plasma">
  <h2><a href="?palette/plasma">Plasma</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from deep blue through magenta and orange to yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="puor">
  <h2><a href="?palette/puor">PuOr</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale PuOr of eleven classes, from a dark brown through orange to a light grey middle and on through lilac to a dark purple (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org), in ColorBrewer&#039;s order, its orange end first: orange against purple, two hues red-green colour-blind eyes still tell apart, and ColorBrewer marks it safe for them. From colorbrewer_schemes.js of ColorBrewer 2.0 (axismaps/colorbrewer), checked against d3-scale-chromatic, which lists it from its purple end. Apache-2.0 licence: this product includes color specifications and designs developed by Cynthia Brewer (http://colorbrewer.org/).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="rdbu">
  <h2><a href="?palette/rdbu">RdBu</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale of eleven classes from dark red through a light grey to dark blue, for values on both sides of a middle - colder and warmer, less and more (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="rdylbu">
  <h2><a href="?palette/rdylbu">RdYlBu</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale RdYlBu of eleven classes, from a dark red through orange to a pale yellow middle and on through light blue to a dark indigo (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org), for values on both sides of a middle - hotter and colder, above and below: the spread of a rainbow, but ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes at every number of classes, where its Spectral and RdYlGn are at most possibly so, and only up to five. From colorbrewer_schemes.js of ColorBrewer 2.0 (axismaps/colorbrewer), checked against d3-scale-chromatic. Apache-2.0 licence: this product includes color specifications and designs developed by Cynthia Brewer (http://colorbrewer.org/).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="romao">
  <h2><a href="?palette/romao">romaO</a></h2>
  <p>Fabio Crameri&#039;s cyclic scale romaO, for what goes round - an angle, a direction, the hour, the month: from a dark plum through brick, ochre and a pale yellow, a pale green, sky blue and indigo back to the plum, its last colour next to its first. Perceptually uniform, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are. The 256 colours of romaO.txt in his Scientific colour maps 8.0.1 (Zenodo, doi:10.5281/zenodo.8409685), each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded. Copyright (c) 2020 Fabio Crameri, MIT licence.</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">

<section class="sx-kind" id="tol-bright">
  <h2><a href="?palette/tol-bright">Tol bright</a></h2>
  <p>Paul Tol&#039;s bright qualitative scheme of seven colours, told apart by colour-blind eyes and in print: blue, red, green, yellow, cyan, purple and grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="tol-muted">
  <h2><a href="?palette/tol-muted">Tol muted</a></h2>
  <p>Paul Tol&#039;s muted qualitative scheme of ten colours, softer than the bright one and still told apart by colour-blind eyes: rose, indigo, sand, green, cyan, wine, teal, olive, purple and a pale grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="tol-vibrant">
  <h2><a href="?palette/tol-vibrant">Tol vibrant</a></h2>
  <p>Paul Tol&#039;s vibrant qualitative scheme of seven colours, made for TensorBoard and told apart by colour-blind eyes: orange, blue, cyan, magenta, red, teal and grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="turbo">
  <h2><a href="?palette/turbo">Turbo</a></h2>
  <p>Google&#039;s rainbow made smooth, by Anton Mikhailov (2019): from a dark indigo through blue, cyan, green, yellow and orange to a dark red, every step a visible change of hue, for a depth map or a field where the eye must read a value at a glance. Its lightness rises to the middle and falls again (CIELAB L* 12 at its start, 91 in the middle, 24 at its end), so it does not print as a ramp in grey, and colour-blind eyes confuse its greens with its reds - for them viridis or batlow. The 256 colours of turbo_srgb_bytes in turbo_colormap.c, the floats of turbo_colormap.py times 255 and rounded, the same floats matplotlib ships as turbo. Copyright 2019 Google LLC, Apache-2.0 licence.</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">

<section class="sx-kind" id="vik">
  <h2><a href="?palette/vik">vik</a></h2>
  <p>Fabio Crameri&#039;s diverging scale vik, from a dark navy through blue and a pale blue to a light middle and on through tan and rust to a dark red-brown, for values on both sides of a middle: perceptually uniform, its two halves nearly alike in lightness, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are. The 256 colours of vik.txt in his Scientific colour maps 8.0.1 (Zenodo, doi:10.5281/zenodo.8409685), each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded. Copyright (c) 2020 Fabio Crameri, MIT licence.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="viridis">
  <h2><a href="?palette/viridis">Viridis</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from dark purple through blue and green to yellow, the default of matplotlib (St&eacute;fan van der Walt and Nathaniel Smith, 2015): even steps look even, it prints in grey as a ramp, and the common forms of colour blindness read it. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">

<section class="sx-kind" id="ylgnbu">
  <h2><a href="?palette/ylgnbu">YlGnBu</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s multi-hue sequential scale YlGnBu of nine classes, from a pale yellow through green and teal to blue and a dark navy (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org): its hue turns as its lightness falls, so the steps of a map or a heatmap tell apart better than in one hue, and ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes. From colorbrewer_schemes.js of ColorBrewer 2.0 (axismaps/colorbrewer), checked against d3-scale-chromatic. Apache-2.0 licence: this product includes color specifications and designs developed by Cynthia Brewer (http://colorbrewer.org/).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="ylorrd">
  <h2><a href="?palette/ylorrd">YlOrRd</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s multi-hue sequential scale YlOrRd of nine classes, from a pale yellow through orange and red to a dark crimson (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org), for heat, risk or intensity: its hue turns as its lightness falls, and ColorBrewer marks it safe for colour-blind eyes. From colorbrewer_schemes.js of ColorBrewer 2.0 (axismaps/colorbrewer), checked against d3-scale-chromatic. Apache-2.0 licence: this product includes color specifications and designs developed by Cynthia Brewer (http://colorbrewer.org/).</p>
